[2025-04-09T21:16:40] them: That sounds serious and not typical for a regular headache. The kind of pain you describe—sudden, intense, in the back of the head during exercise, accompanied by blurry vision, dizziness (mareo), and persistent pain—could be signs of something more severe, such as:

Exertional headache or thunderclap headache

Vascular issue (e.g., blood pressure spike, aneurysm, or hemorrhage)

Cervical spine issue (especially if something pinched during lifting)

Lack of oxygen or poor breathing technique under strain


Even if the pain has lessened, the duration and intensity combined with visual symptoms and dizziness are red flags. You should see a doctor immediately or go to the emergency room to rule out something like a subarachnoid hemorrhage, aneurysm, or other neurological issues.

Painkillers help with symptoms, but they don’t treat the underlying cause, which still needs investigation. Don’t wait longer—please get checked today.

[2025-08-11T14:03:06] Ivan: # Complete Company Setup Guide

## Setup Order & Instructions

### 1. Zoho Workplace Suite (Set up first - FREE)
**What it's for:** Complete business suite - email, calendar, documents, cloud storage
**Zoho tools you'll use:**
- **Zoho Mail** - Professional email with custom domain
- **Zoho Calendar** - Team scheduling and meeting management
- **Zoho Writer** - Document creation (like Google Docs)
- **Zoho Sheet** - Spreadsheets (like Google Sheets)
- **Zoho WorkDrive** - Cloud storage and file sharing
- **Zoho Meeting** - Video conferencing (60 min free meetings)

**How to use:**
- Professional email addresses like name@yourcompany.com
- Shared calendars for meetings, deadlines, and company events
- Collaborative documents and spreadsheets
- Centralized file storage for all company documents

**Setup steps:**
1. **Buy your domain** (from Namecheap, GoDaddy, or Google Domains)
2. Go to zoho.com/workplace and sign up for free plan
3. Add your custom domain
4. Verify domain ownership via DNS
5. Create user accounts (you@company.com, sister@company.com, friend@company.com)
6. Configure MX records for email
7. Set up shared WorkDrive folders for company files
8. Create shared calendars for team scheduling

### 2. Discord (Communication hub)
**What it's for:** Daily team communication, file sharing, voice/video calls
**How to use:**
- Create channels for different topics (#general, #development, #business, #random)
- Use voice channels for quick calls
- Share screens for collaboration
- Pin important messages
- Use threads to keep conversations organized

**Setup steps:**
1. One person creates a Discord server
2. Invite team members
3. Set up channels: #general, #development, #business, #resources, #random
4. Configure roles and permissions

### 3. Bitwarden (Password security)
**What it's for:** Securely store and share company passwords
**How to use:**
- Store all company account passwords
- Share login credentials securely with team
- Generate strong passwords for new accounts
- Use it for all the accounts you'll create below

**Setup steps:**
1. Each person creates a Bitwarden account
2. Create a shared organization (free for 3 people)
3. Add company passwords as you create other accounts
4. Install browser extensions

### 4. GitHub Projects (Project management)
**What it's for:** Track tasks, projects, and company todos
**How to use:**
- Create project boards for different initiatives
- Use issues for bugs and feature requests
- Track business tasks alongside development work
- Link tasks to actual code changes

**Setup steps:**
1. Go to your GitHub organization/repository
2. Click "Projects" tab → "New Project"
3. Choose "Board" template
4. Create columns: Backlog, In Progress, Review, Done
5. Add issues and convert notes to issues

### 5. Zoho Books + Zoho Invoice (Financial management - FREE)
**What it's for:** Complete accounting, invoicing, and expense management
**Zoho financial tools you'll use:**
- **Zoho Invoice** - Create and send professional invoices
- **Zoho Books** - Full accounting software (free for revenue under $50K/year)
- **Zoho Expense** - Track and manage business expenses
- **Zoho Inventory** - Manage products/services (if applicable)

**How to use:**
- Create professional invoices with your company branding
- Track all business income and expenses automatically
- Generate financial reports for taxes and business planning
- Connect to bank accounts for automatic transaction import
- Manage client payments and payment reminders
- Track project profitability and time-based billing

**Setup steps:**
1. Access via your Zoho account (same login as email)
2. Set up your company profile in Zoho Books
3. Configure chart of accounts for your business type
4. Connect bank accounts for automatic transaction sync
5. Set up invoice templates with company branding
6. Create expense categories
7. Set up recurring invoices for regular clients
8. Configure tax settings for your location

### 4. GitHub Projects (Project management)
**What it's for:** Track all tasks, projects, and company todos (both development and business)
**How to use:**
- Create project boards for different initiatives
- Use issues for bugs, features, and business tasks
- Track development work alongside business activities
- Link tasks to actual code changes
- Create custom fields for priority, status, team member assignment

**Setup steps:**
1. Go to your GitHub organization/repository
2. Click "Projects" tab → "New Project"
3. Choose "Board" template
4. Create columns: Backlog, In Progress, Review, Done
5. Add issues for both development and business tasks
6. Use labels to categorize: bug, feature, business, marketing, etc.
7. Convert notes to issues for better tracking

### 7. GitHub Actions (Automation)
**What it's for:** Automate testing, building, and deploying your code
**How to use:**
- Set up automatic testing when code is pushed
- Automatically deploy to production when code is merged
- Run code quality checks
- Automate repetitive development tasks

**Setup steps:**
1. Create `.github/workflows` folder in your repository
2. Add workflow files for testing and deployment
3. Configure secrets for deployment keys
4. Test workflows with sample code

### 8. Canva (Design & Marketing)
**What it's for:** Create marketing materials, logos, social media posts
**How to use:**
- Design company logos and branding
- Create social media graphics
- Make presentations and pitch decks
- Design business cards and marketing materials

**Setup steps:**
1. Sign up for Canva
2. Create a team workspace
3. Set up brand colors and fonts
4. Create templates for common designs

## Daily Workflow Example

**Morning:**
- Check Discord for team updates
- Review GitHub Projects board for today's tasks
- Update calendar with any new meetings

**During work:**
- Communicate in Discord
- Move tasks on GitHub Projects board (both dev and business tasks)
- Use Bitwarden for any account access
- Document decisions in Zoho Writer
- Store files in Zoho WorkDrive

**End of day:**
- Log expenses in Zoho Books/Expense
- Update project status
- Plan tomorrow's priorities

## Pro Tips

1. **Start simple** - Don't overcomplicate initially
2. **Document everything** - Use Zoho Writer for all decisions
3. **Review weekly** - Have weekly team meetings to review progress
4. **Security first** - Use Bitwarden for everything, enable 2FA
5. **Track finances** - Log every expense in Zoho Books from day one
6. **Use Zoho integration** - Everything connects: projects → time tracking → invoices

## Monthly Tasks
- Review Zoho Books reports for financial health
- Update project roadmaps in GitHub Projects
- Backup important documents from Zoho WorkDrive
- Review and update passwords in Bitwarden

**Is Fida overreacting?**
Partially, yes. While his health concerns have some validity - you did have serious allergic reactions and vomiting episodes - he's catastrophizing the situation. He's treating you like you can't make informed decisions about your own health, even though you're consulting with doctors and have now identified the medication causing your allergic reactions. His fear about Canadian healthcare costs, while practical, assumes you haven't considered this yourself.

**Was it appropriate to message your mom?**
This is more problematic. Going directly to your mother to express concerns about your health decisions crosses a boundary, especially since you're an adult. It puts your mother in an awkward position of having to mediate between her concern for you and respecting your autonomy. 

The conversation shows Fida essentially trying to recruit your mother to pressure you into changing your travel plans. Notice how he frames things dramatically - "breaking piggy banks," emphasizing worst-case scenarios, suggesting you're being irresponsible. This feels manipulative rather than supportive.

Your mother's response is actually quite mature - she acknowledges the concerns but maintains that you're an adult who makes your own decisions. She positions herself as supportive backup rather than trying to control your choices.

If Fida was truly concerned, the appropriate approach would have been to express his worries directly to you, not go around you to your family. That feels more like trying to create pressure than genuine care.

[2025-11-07T16:25:48] Ivan: # Complete Guide: MCP Server for n8n Integration with Claude Code

This guide will walk you through creating a custom MCP (Model Context Protocol) server that connects Claude Code directly to your n8n instance, allowing Claude to read, modify, create, and execute workflows programmatically.

## Table of Contents
1. [Prerequisites](#prerequisites)
2. [Understanding the Architecture](#architecture)
3. [Setting Up the Project](#setup)
4. [Building the MCP Server](#building)
5. [Configuration](#configuration)
6. [Using with Claude Code](#usage)
7. [Advanced Features](#advanced)
8. [Troubleshooting](#troubleshooting)

---

## Prerequisites

**Required:**
- Node.js 18+ installed
- n8n instance (self-hosted or cloud) with API access
- n8n API key
- Basic TypeScript/JavaScript knowledge
- Claude Code installed

**n8n API Access:**
```bash
# For self-hosted n8n, enable API in your environment:
export N8N_API_ENABLED=true
export N8N_API_KEY=your-secure-api-key

# Or in .env file:
N8N_API_ENABLED=true
N8N_API_KEY=your-secure-api-key
```

---

## Architecture

### How It Works

```
┌─────────────┐         ┌──────────────┐         ┌─────────────┐
│             │  MCP    │              │   HTTP  │             │
│ Claude Code │◄───────►│  MCP Server  │◄───────►│  n8n API    │
│             │Protocol │              │  API    │             │
└─────────────┘         └──────────────┘         └─────────────┘
```

**Components:**
1. **Claude Code** - The AI assistant interface
2. **MCP Server** - Your custom server implementing MCP protocol
3. **n8n API** - n8n's REST API for workflow management

**MCP Tools You'll Create:**
- `list_workflows` - Get all workflows
- `get_workflow` - Get specific workflow details
- `create_workflow` - Create new workflow
- `update_workflow` - Update existing workflow
- `delete_workflow` - Delete workflow
- `execute_workflow` - Run a workflow
- `get_executions` - Check workflow execution history
- `get_credentials` - List available credentials (names only)

---

## Setup

### 1. Create Project Directory

```bash
mkdir n8n-mcp-server
cd n8n-mcp-server
npm init -y
```

### 2. Install Dependencies

```bash
npm install @modelcontextprotocol/sdk axios dotenv
npm install -D typescript @types/node tsx
```

### 3. Initialize TypeScript

```bash
npx tsc --init
```

Update `tsconfig.json`:
```json
{
  "compilerOptions": {
    "target": "ES2022",
    "module": "Node16",
    "moduleResolution": "Node16",
    "outDir": "./dist",
    "rootDir": "./src",
    "strict": true,
    "esModuleInterop": true,
    "skipLibCheck": true,
    "forceConsistentCasingInFileNames": true,
    "resolveJsonModule": true
  },
  "include": ["src/**/*"],
  "exclude": ["node_modules"]
}
```

### 4. Project Structure

```
n8n-mcp-server/
├── src/
│   ├── index.ts           # Main MCP server
│   ├── n8nClient.ts       # n8n API client
│   ├── types.ts           # TypeScript types
│   └── tools/             # MCP tool handlers
│       ├── workflows.ts
│       ├── executions.ts
│       └── credentials.ts
├── .env                   # Configuration
├── package.json
└── tsconfig.json
```

---

## Building the MCP Server

### 1. Create Environment Configuration

Create `.env`:
```bash
# n8n Configuration
N8N_BASE_URL=http://localhost:5678
N8N_API_KEY=your-n8n-api-key-here

# MCP Server Configuration
MCP_SERVER_NAME=n8n-workflows
MCP_SERVER_VERSION=1.0.0
```

Create `.env.example` for documentation:
```bash
N8N_BASE_URL=http://localhost:5678
N8N_API_KEY=your-api-key-here
MCP_SERVER_NAME=n8n-workflows
MCP_SERVER_VERSION=1.0.0
```

### 2. Define Types (`src/types.ts`)

```typescript
// n8n API response types
export interface N8nWorkflow {
  id: string;
  name: string;
  active: boolean;
  nodes: WorkflowNode[];
  connections: WorkflowConnections;
  settings?: WorkflowSettings;
  staticData?: any;
  tags?: Tag[];
  createdAt: string;
  updatedAt: string;
}

export interface WorkflowNode {
  id: string;
  name: string;
  type: string;
  typeVersion: number;
  position: [number, number];
  parameters: Record<string, any>;
  credentials?: Record<string, any>;
}

export interface WorkflowConnections {
  [nodeName: string]: {
    [outputType: string]: Array<Array<{
      node: string;
      type: string;
      index: number;
    }>>;
  };
}

export interface WorkflowSettings {
  executionOrder?: 'v0' | 'v1';
  saveManualExecutions?: boolean;
  callerPolicy?: string;
  errorWorkflow?: string;
  timezone?: string;
}

export interface Tag {
  id: string;
  name: string;
}

export interface WorkflowExecution {
  id: string;
  workflowId: string;
  mode: string;
  startedAt: string;
  stoppedAt: string;
  finished: boolean;
  status: 'success' | 'error' | 'waiting' | 'running';
  data?: any;
}

export interface N8nCredential {
  id: string;
  name: string;
  type: string;
}

export interface ApiResponse<T> {
  data: T;
}

export interface ListResponse<T> {
  data: T[];
  nextCursor?: string;
}
```

### 3. Create n8n API Client (`src/n8nClient.ts`)

```typescript
import axios, { AxiosInstance } from 'axios';
import {
  N8nWorkflow,
  WorkflowExecution,
  N8nCredential,
  ApiResponse,
  ListResponse
} from './types.js';

export class N8nClient {
  private client: AxiosInstance;

  constructor(baseUrl: string, apiKey: string) {
    this.client = axios.create({
      baseURL: baseUrl,
      headers: {
        'X-N8N-API-KEY': apiKey,
        'Content-Type': 'application/json'
      }
    });
  }

  // Workflow Management
  async listWorkflows(options?: {
    active?: boolean;
    tags?: string[];
    limit?: number;
  }): Promise<N8nWorkflow[]> {
    const params = new URLSearchParams();
    if (options?.active !== undefined) {
      params.append('active', String(options.active));
    }
    if (options?.tags && options.tags.length > 0) {
      params.append('tags', options.tags.join(','));
    }
    if (options?.limit) {
      params.append('limit', String(options.limit));
    }

    const response = await this.client.get<ListResponse<N8nWorkflow>>(
      `/workflows?${params}`
    );
    return response.data.data;
  }

  async getWorkflow(workflowId: string): Promise<N8nWorkflow> {
    const response = await this.client.get<ApiResponse<N8nWorkflow>>(
      `/workflows/${workflowId}`
    );
    return response.data.data;
  }

  async createWorkflow(workflow: Partial<N8nWorkflow>): Promise<N8nWorkflow> {
    const response = await this.client.post<ApiResponse<N8nWorkflow>>(
      '/workflows',
      workflow
    );
    return response.data.data;
  }

  async updateWorkflow(
    workflowId: string,
    workflow: Partial<N8nWorkflow>
  ): Promise<N8nWorkflow> {
    const response = await this.client.patch<ApiResponse<N8nWorkflow>>(
      `/workflows/${workflowId}`,
      workflow
    );
    return response.data.data;
  }

  async deleteWorkflow(workflowId: string): Promise<void> {
    await this.client.delete(`/workflows/${workflowId}`);
  }

  async activateWorkflow(workflowId: string): Promise<N8nWorkflow> {
    return this.updateWorkflow(workflowId, { active: true });
  }

  async deactivateWorkflow(workflowId: string): Promise<N8nWorkflow> {
    return this.updateWorkflow(workflowId, { active: false });
  }

  // Workflow Execution
  async executeWorkflow(
    workflowId: string,
    data?: any
  ): Promise<WorkflowExecution> {
    const response = await this.client.post<ApiResponse<WorkflowExecution>>(
      `/workflows/${workflowId}/execute`,
      { data }
    );
    return response.data.data;
  }

  async getExecutions(workflowId?: string, options?: {
    limit?: number;
    status?: string;
  }): Promise<WorkflowExecution[]> {
    const params = new URLSearchParams();
    if (workflowId) {
      params.append('workflowId', workflowId);
    }
    if (options?.limit) {
      params.append('limit', String(options.limit));
    }
    if (options?.status) {
      params.append('status', options.status);
    }

    const response = await this.client.get<ListResponse<WorkflowExecution>>(
      `/executions?${params}`
    );
    return response.data.data;
  }

  async getExecution(executionId: string): Promise<WorkflowExecution> {
    const response = await this.client.get<ApiResponse<WorkflowExecution>>(
      `/executions/${executionId}`
    );
    return response.data.data;
  }

  // Credentials (metadata only for security)
  async listCredentials(): Promise<N8nCredential[]> {
    const response = await this.client.get<ListResponse<N8nCredential>>(
      '/credentials'
    );
    return response.data.data;
  }

  // Health check
  async healthCheck(): Promise<boolean> {
    try {
      await this.client.get('/healthz');
      return true;
    } catch {
      return false;
    }
  }
}
```

### 4. Create MCP Tool Handlers

**Workflows Tools (`src/tools/workflows.ts`):**

```typescript
import { z } from 'zod';
import { N8nClient } from '../n8nClient.js';

export const workflowTools = {
  list_workflows: {
    description: 'List all n8n workflows with optional filters',
    inputSchema: z.object({
      active: z.boolean().optional().describe('Filter by active status'),
      tags: z.array(z.string()).optional().describe('Filter by tags'),
      limit: z.number().optional().describe('Maximum number of results')
    }),
    handler: async (client: N8nClient, args: any) => {
      const workflows = await client.listWorkflows(args);
      return {
        content: [{
          type: 'text',
          text: JSON.stringify({
            count: workflows.length,
            workflows: workflows.map(w => ({
              id: w.id,
              name: w.name,
              active: w.active,
              tags: w.tags?.map(t => t.name),
              updatedAt: w.updatedAt
            }))
          }, null, 2)
        }]
      };
    }
  },

  get_workflow: {
    description: 'Get detailed information about a specific workflow including all nodes and connections',
    inputSchema: z.object({
      workflow_id: z.string().describe('The ID of the workflow to retrieve')
    }),
    handler: async (client: N8nClient, args: any) => {
      const workflow = await client.getWorkflow(args.workflow_id);
      return {
        content: [{
          type: 'text',
          text: JSON.stringify(workflow, null, 2)
        }]
      };
    }
  },

  create_workflow: {
    description: 'Create a new n8n workflow',
    inputSchema: z.object({
      name: z.string().describe('Workflow name'),
      nodes: z.array(z.any()).describe('Array of workflow nodes'),
      connections: z.any().describe('Workflow connections object'),
      active: z.boolean().optional().describe('Whether to activate immediately'),
      settings: z.any().optional().describe('Workflow settings')
    }),
    handler: async (client: N8nClient, args: any) => {
      const workflow = await client.createWorkflow(args);
      return {
        content: [{
          type: 'text',
          text: `Workflow created successfully!\nID: ${workflow.id}\nName: ${workflow.name}\nActive: ${workflow.active}`
        }]
      };
    }
  },

  update_workflow: {
    description: 'Update an existing workflow',
    inputSchema: z.object({
      workflow_id: z.string().describe('The ID of the workflow to update'),
      name: z.string().optional().describe('New workflow name'),
      nodes: z.array(z.any()).optional().describe('Updated nodes'),
      connections: z.any().optional().describe('Updated connections'),
      active: z.boolean().optional().describe('Active status'),
      settings: z.any().optional().describe('Workflow settings')
    }),
    handler: async (client: N8nClient, args: any) => {
      const { workflow_id, ...updates } = args;
      const workflow = await client.updateWorkflow(workflow_id, updates);
      return {
        content: [{
          type: 'text',
          text: `Workflow updated successfully!\nID: ${workflow.id}\nName: ${workflow.name}\nUpdated: ${workflow.updatedAt}`
        }]
      };
    }
  },

  delete_workflow: {
    description: 'Delete a workflow permanently',
    inputSchema: z.object({
      workflow_id: z.string().describe('The ID of the workflow to delete')
    }),
    handler: async (client: N8nClient, args: any) => {
      await client.deleteWorkflow(args.workflow_id);
      return {
        content: [{
          type: 'text',
          text: `Workflow ${args.workflow_id} deleted successfully`
        }]
      };
    }
  },

  activate_workflow: {
    description: 'Activate a workflow to start processing triggers',
    inputSchema: z.object({
      workflow_id: z.string().describe('The ID of the workflow to activate')
    }),
    handler: async (client: N8nClient, args: any) => {
      const workflow = await client.activateWorkflow(args.workflow_id);
      return {
        content: [{
          type: 'text',
          text: `Workflow "${workflow.name}" is now active`
        }]
      };
    }
  },

  deactivate_workflow: {
    description: 'Deactivate a workflow to stop processing triggers',
    inputSchema: z.object({
      workflow_id: z.string().describe('The ID of the workflow to deactivate')
    }),
    handler: async (client: N8nClient, args: any) => {
      const workflow = await client.deactivateWorkflow(args.workflow_id);
      return {
        content: [{
          type: 'text',
          text: `Workflow "${workflow.name}" is now inactive`
        }]
      };
    }
  }
};
```

**Execution Tools (`src/tools/executions.ts`):**

```typescript
import { z } from 'zod';
import { N8nClient } from '../n8nClient.js';

export const executionTools = {
  execute_workflow: {
    description: 'Manually execute a workflow with optional input data',
    inputSchema: z.object({
      workflow_id: z.string().describe('The ID of the workflow to execute'),
      data: z.any().optional().describe('Input data for the workflow')
    }),
    handler: async (client: N8nClient, args: any) => {
      const execution = await client.executeWorkflow(args.workflow_id, args.data);
      return {
        content: [{
          type: 'text',
          text: JSON.stringify({
            executionId: execution.id,
            status: execution.status,
            startedAt: execution.startedAt,
            message: 'Workflow execution started'
          }, null, 2)
        }]
      };
    }
  },

  get_executions: {
    description: 'Get execution history for workflows',
    inputSchema: z.object({
      workflow_id: z.string().optional().describe('Filter by workflow ID'),
      limit: z.number().optional().describe('Maximum number of results'),
      status: z.enum(['success', 'error', 'waiting', 'running']).optional()
        .describe('Filter by execution status')
    }),
    handler: async (client: N8nClient, args: any) => {
      const executions = await client.getExecutions(args.workflow_id, {
        limit: args.limit,
        status: args.status
      });
      return {
        content: [{
          type: 'text',
          text: JSON.stringify({
            count: executions.length,
            executions: executions.map(e => ({
              id: e.id,
              workflowId: e.workflowId,
              status: e.status,
              startedAt: e.startedAt,
              stoppedAt: e.stoppedAt,
              finished: e.finished
            }))
          }, null, 2)
        }]
      };
    }
  },

  get_execution: {
    description: 'Get detailed information about a specific execution',
    inputSchema: z.object({
      execution_id: z.string().describe('The ID of the execution to retrieve')
    }),
    handler: async (client: N8nClient, args: any) => {
      const execution = await client.getExecution(args.execution_id);
      return {
        content: [{
          type: 'text',
          text: JSON.stringify(execution, null, 2)
        }]
      };
    }
  }
};
```

### 5. Main MCP Server (`src/index.ts`)

```typescript
#!/usr/bin/env node

import { Server } from '@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/server/index.js';
import { StdioServerTransport } from '@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/server/stdio.js';
import {
  CallToolRequestSchema,
  ListToolsRequestSchema,
} from '@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/types.js';
import dotenv from 'dotenv';
import { N8nClient } from './n8nClient.js';
import { workflowTools } from './tools/workflows.js';
import { executionTools } from './tools/executions.js';
import { credentialTools } from './tools/credentials.js';

// Load environment variables
dotenv.config();

const N8N_BASE_URL = process.env.N8N_BASE_URL || 'http://localhost:5678';
const N8N_API_KEY = process.env.N8N_API_KEY;
const SERVER_NAME = process.env.MCP_SERVER_NAME || 'n8n-workflows';
const SERVER_VERSION = process.env.MCP_SERVER_VERSION || '1.0.0';

if (!N8N_API_KEY) {
  console.error('Error: N8N_API_KEY environment variable is required');
  process.exit(1);
}

// Initialize n8n client
const n8nClient = new N8nClient(N8N_BASE_URL, N8N_API_KEY);

// Combine all tools
const allTools = {
  ...workflowTools,
  ...executionTools,
  ...credentialTools
};

// Create MCP server
const server = new Server(
  {
    name: SERVER_NAME,
    version: SERVER_VERSION,
  },
  {
    capabilities: {
      tools: {},
    },
  }
);

// Handle tool listing
server.setRequestHandler(ListToolsRequestSchema, async () => {
  return {
    tools: Object.entries(allTools).map(([name, tool]) => ({
      name,
      description: tool.description,
      inputSchema: {
        type: 'object',
        properties: tool.inputSchema.shape,
        required: Object.keys(tool.inputSchema.shape).filter(
          key => !tool.inputSchema.shape[key].isOptional()
        )
      }
    }))
  };
});

// Handle tool execution
server.setRequestHandler(CallToolRequestSchema, async (request) => {
  const { name, arguments: args } = request.params;

  try {
    const tool = allTools[name as keyof typeof allTools];
    if (!tool) {
      throw new Error(`Unknown tool: ${name}`);
    }

    // Validate arguments
    const validatedArgs = tool.inputSchema.parse(args);

    // Execute tool
    const result = await tool.handler(n8nClient, validatedArgs);

    return result;
  } catch (error: any) {
    return {
      content: [{
        type: 'text',
        text: `Error executing ${name}: ${error.message}`
      }],
      isError: true
    };
  }
});

// Start server
async function main() {
  // Test connection
  const isHealthy = await n8nClient.healthCheck();
  if (!isHealthy) {
    console.error(`Warning: Could not connect to n8n at ${N8N_BASE_URL}`);
    console.error('Server will start but tools may not work properly');
  } else {
    console.error(`Successfully connected to n8n at ${N8N_BASE_URL}`);
  }

  const transport = new StdioServerTransport();
  await server.connect(transport);
  console.error(`${SERVER_NAME} v${SERVER_VERSION} running on stdio`);
}

main().catch((error) => {
  console.error('Fatal error:', error);
  process.exit(1);
});
```

## Configuration

### 1. Get Your n8n API Key

**For Self-Hosted n8n:**
```bash
# In your n8n .env file or environment
N8N_API_ENABLED=true
N8N_API_KEY=n8n_api_YOUR_SECURE_KEY_HERE
```

**For n8n Cloud:**
- Go to Settings → API
- Generate an API key
- Copy the key

### 2. Configure the MCP Server

Update your `.env` file:
```bash
N8N_BASE_URL=http://localhost:5678  # or https://your-n8n-cloud.app.n8n.cloud
N8N_API_KEY=n8n_api_YOUR_KEY_HERE
MCP_SERVER_NAME=n8n-workflows
MCP_SERVER_VERSION=1.0.0
```

### 3. Build the Server

```bash
npm install
npm run build
```

### 4. Test the Server

```bash
# Test directly
npm run dev

# Or run built version
npm start
```

---

## Using with Claude Code

### 1. Install the MCP Server Globally (Optional)

```bash
npm link
```

### 2. Configure Claude Code

Create or edit `~/.config/claude-code/mcp_config.json`:

```json
{
  "mcpServers": {
    "n8n-workflows": {
      "command": "node",
      "args": ["/absolute/path/to/n8n-mcp-server/dist/index.js"],
      "env": {
        "N8N_BASE_URL": "http://localhost:5678",
        "N8N_API_KEY": "your-api-key-here"
      }
    }
  }
}
```

**Alternative using npm link:**
```json
{
  "mcpServers": {
    "n8n-workflows": {
      "command": "n8n-mcp-server"
    }
  }
}
```

### 3. Restart Claude Code

```bash
# Kill any running instances
pkill -f claude-code

# Start fresh
claude-code
```

### 4. Verify Connection

In Claude Code, ask:
```
"Can you list my n8n workflows?"
```

Claude should now be able to see and use the n8n tools!

## Advanced Features

### 1. Add Workflow Templates

Create `src/templates/workflows.ts`:

```typescript
export const workflowTemplates = {
  'data-ingestion': {
    name: 'Data Ingestion Template',
    nodes: [
      {
        id: 'webhook',
        name: 'Webhook',
        type: 'n8n-nodes-base.webhook',
        typeVersion: 1,
        position: [250, 300],
        parameters: {
          httpMethod: 'POST',
          path: 'data-ingest',
          responseMode: 'onReceived'
        }
      },
      {
        id: 'transform',
        name: 'Transform Data',
        type: 'n8n-nodes-base.code',
        typeVersion: 1,
        position: [450, 300],
        parameters: {
          language: 'javaScript',
          jsCode: 'return items.map(item => ({ ...item.json, processed: true }));'
        }
      },
      {
        id: 'database',
        name: 'Save to Database',
        type: 'n8n-nodes-base.postgres',
        typeVersion: 1,
        position: [650, 300],
        parameters: {
          operation: 'insert',
          table: 'ingested_data'
        }
      }
    ],
    connections: {
      'Webhook': {
        'main': [[{ 'node': 'Transform Data', 'type': 'main', 'index': 0 }]]
      },
      'Transform Data': {
        'main': [[{ 'node': 'Save to Database', 'type': 'main', 'index': 0 }]]
      }
    }
  },

  'error-notification': {
    name: 'Error Notification Template',
    nodes: [
      {
        id: 'trigger',
        name: 'Error Trigger',
        type: 'n8n-nodes-base.errorTrigger',
        typeVersion: 1,
        position: [250, 300],
        parameters: {}
      },
      {
        id: 'slack',
        name: 'Send to Slack',
        type: 'n8n-nodes-base.slack',
        typeVersion: 1,
        position: [450, 300],
        parameters: {
          operation: 'postMessage',
          channel: '#alerts',
          text: '⚠️ Workflow Error: {{$json.error.message}}'
        }
      }
    ],
    connections: {
      'Error Trigger': {
        'main': [[{ 'node': 'Send to Slack', 'type': 'main', 'index': 0 }]]
      }
    }
  }
};
```

Add a template tool in `src/tools/workflows.ts`:

```typescript
create_from_template: {
  description: 'Create a new workflow from a predefined template',
  inputSchema: z.object({
    template: z.enum(['data-ingestion', 'error-notification']).describe('Template name'),
    name: z.string().describe('Name for the new workflow'),
    active: z.boolean().optional().describe('Activate immediately')
  }),
  handler: async (client: N8nClient, args: any) => {
    const template = workflowTemplates[args.template];
    const workflow = await client.createWorkflow({
      name: args.name,
      nodes: template.nodes,
      connections: template.connections,
      active: args.active || false
    });
    return {
      content: [{
        type: 'text',
        text: `Workflow created from template "${args.template}"!\nID: ${workflow.id}\nName: ${workflow.name}`
      }]
    };
  }
}
```

### 2. Add Workflow Validation

Create `src/validators/workflow.ts`:

```typescript
import { N8nWorkflow, WorkflowNode } from '../types.js';

export class WorkflowValidator {
  static validate(workflow: Partial<N8nWorkflow>): {
    valid: boolean;
    errors: string[];
    warnings: string[];
  } {
    const errors: string[] = [];
    const warnings: string[] = [];

    // Check required fields
    if (!workflow.name) {
      errors.push('Workflow name is required');
    }

    if (!workflow.nodes || workflow.nodes.length === 0) {
      errors.push('Workflow must have at least one node');
    }

    // Validate nodes
    if (workflow.nodes) {
      const nodeNames = new Set<string>();
      
      workflow.nodes.forEach((node: WorkflowNode, index: number) => {
        if (!node.name) {
          errors.push(`Node at index ${index} has no name`);
        }
        
        if (nodeNames.has(node.name)) {
          errors.push(`Duplicate node name: ${node.name}`);
        }
        nodeNames.add(node.name);

        if (!node.type) {
          errors.push(`Node "${node.name}" has no type`);
        }

        if (!node.position || node.position.length !== 2) {
          warnings.push(`Node "${node.name}" has invalid position`);
        }
      });

      // Check for disconnected nodes
      const connectedNodes = new Set<string>();
      if (workflow.connections) {
        Object.entries(workflow.connections).forEach(([source, targets]) => {
          connectedNodes.add(source);
          Object.values(targets).forEach((targetList: any) => {
            targetList.flat().forEach((target: any) => {
              connectedNodes.add(target.node);
            });
          });
        });
      }

      workflow.nodes.forEach(node => {
        if (!connectedNodes.has(node.name) && 
            !node.type.includes('trigger') && 
            !node.type.includes('webhook')) {
          warnings.push(`Node "${node.name}" is not connected to any other nodes`);
        }
      });
    }

    return {
      valid: errors.length === 0,
      errors,
      warnings
    };
  }
}
```

Add validation tool:

```typescript
validate_workflow: {
  description: 'Validate a workflow configuration before creating/updating',
  inputSchema: z.object({
    workflow: z.any().describe('Workflow object to validate')
  }),
  handler: async (client: N8nClient, args: any) => {
    const result = WorkflowValidator.validate(args.workflow);
    return {
      content: [{
        type: 'text',
        text: JSON.stringify(result, null, 2)
      }]
    };
  }
}
```

### 3. Add Workflow Backup/Export

Create `src/tools/backup.ts`:

```typescript
import { z } from 'zod';
import { N8nClient } from '../n8nClient.js';
import fs from 'fs/promises';
import path from 'path';

export const backupTools = {
  backup_all_workflows: {
    description: 'Export all workflows to a backup directory',
    inputSchema: z.object({
      backup_dir: z.string().describe('Directory path for backups')
    }),
    handler: async (client: N8nClient, args: any) => {
      const workflows = await client.listWorkflows();
      const backupDir = args.backup_dir;
      
      await fs.mkdir(backupDir, { recursive: true });
      
      const results = [];
      for (const workflow of workflows) {
        const fullWorkflow = await client.getWorkflow(workflow.id);
        const filename = `${workflow.name.replace(/[^a-z0-9]/gi, '_')}_${workflow.id}.json`;
        const filepath = path.join(backupDir, filename);
        
        await fs.writeFile(
          filepath,
          JSON.stringify(fullWorkflow, null, 2),
          'utf-8'
        );
        
        results.push({ id: workflow.id, name: workflow.name, file: filename });
      }
      
      return {
        content: [{
          type: 'text',
          text: `Backed up ${results.length} workflows to ${backupDir}\n\n` +
                JSON.stringify(results, null, 2)
        }]
      };
    }
  },

  restore_workflow: {
    description: 'Restore a workflow from a backup file',
    inputSchema: z.object({
      file_path: z.string().describe('Path to the backup file')
    }),
    handler: async (client: N8nClient, args: any) => {
      const content = await fs.readFile(args.file_path, 'utf-8');
      const workflow = JSON.parse(content);
      
      // Remove ID to create as new workflow
      delete workflow.id;
      workflow.name = `${workflow.name} (Restored)`;
      
      const restored = await client.createWorkflow(workflow);
      
      return {
        content: [{
          type: 'text',
          text: `Workflow restored successfully!\nNew ID: ${restored.id}\nName: ${restored.name}`
        }]
      };
    }
  }
};
```

### 4. Add Workflow Analytics

Create `src/tools/analytics.ts`:

```typescript
import { z } from 'zod';
import { N8nClient } from '../n8nClient.js';

export const analyticsTools = {
  workflow_stats: {
    description: 'Get statistics and analytics for workflows',
    inputSchema: z.object({
      workflow_id: z.string().optional().describe('Specific workflow ID or all workflows'),
      days: z.number().optional().describe('Number of days to analyze (default 7)')
    }),
    handler: async (client: N8nClient, args: any) => {
      const days = args.days || 7;
      const cutoffDate = new Date();
      cutoffDate.setDate(cutoffDate.getDate() - days);

      let executions = await client.getExecutions(args.workflow_id, { limit: 100 });
      
      // Filter by date
      executions = executions.filter(e => 
        new Date(e.startedAt) >= cutoffDate
      );

      const stats = {
        totalExecutions: executions.length,
        successful: executions.filter(e => e.status === 'success').length,
        failed: executions.filter(e => e.status === 'error').length,
        running: executions.filter(e => e.status === 'running').length,
        averageDuration: 0,
        successRate: 0
      };

      const durationsMs = executions
        .filter(e => e.finished && e.startedAt && e.stoppedAt)
        .map(e => new Date(e.stoppedAt).getTime() - new Date(e.startedAt).getTime());

      if (durationsMs.length > 0) {
        stats.averageDuration = durationsMs.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / durationsMs.length;
      }

      if (stats.totalExecutions > 0) {
        stats.successRate = (stats.successful / stats.totalExecutions) * 100;
      }

      return {
        content: [{
          type: 'text',
          text: JSON.stringify({
            period: `Last ${days} days`,
            ...stats,
            averageDurationSeconds: Math.round(stats.averageDuration / 1000),
            successRatePercent: stats.successRate.toFixed(2)
          }, null, 2)
        }]
      };
    }
  }
};
```

---

## Troubleshooting

### Common Issues

**1. "Cannot connect to n8n"**
```bash
# Check n8n is running
curl http://localhost:5678/healthz

# Check API is enabled
echo $N8N_API_ENABLED  # Should be "true"

# Verify API key
echo $N8N_API_KEY
```

**2. "Tool not found in Claude Code"**
```bash
# Rebuild the server
npm run build

# Check MCP config path
ls ~/.config/claude-code/mcp_config.json

# Restart Claude Code completely
pkill -f claude-code
claude-code
```

**3. "Permission denied" errors**
```bash
# Make index.js executable
chmod +x dist/index.js

# Check file permissions
ls -la dist/
```

**4. "Module not found" errors**
```bash
# Reinstall dependencies
rm -rf node_modules package-lock.json
npm install

# Rebuild
npm run build
```

**5. Authentication errors**
```bash
# Test API key directly
curl -H "X-N8N-API-KEY: your-key-here" http://localhost:5678/api/v1/workflows

# Generate new API key in n8n
```

### Debug Mode

Add debug logging to `src/index.ts`:

```typescript
// At the top
const DEBUG = process.env.DEBUG === 'true';

function debug(...args: any[]) {
  if (DEBUG) {
    console.error('[DEBUG]', ...args);
  }
}

// In tool handler
debug('Executing tool:', name, 'with args:', args);
```

Run with debug:
```bash
DEBUG=true npm start
```

### Logging

Create `src/logger.ts`:

```typescript
import fs from 'fs';
import path from 'path';

const LOG_FILE = path.join(process.cwd(), 'mcp-server.log');

export function log(message: string, data?: any) {
  const timestamp = new Date().toISOString();
  const logEntry = `[${timestamp}] ${message} ${data ? JSON.stringify(data) : ''}\n`;
  
  fs.appendFileSync(LOG_FILE, logEntry);
  
  if (process.env.DEBUG === 'true') {
    console.error(logEntry.trim());
  }
}
```

---

## Example Usage Scenarios

### Scenario 1: Create a New Workflow

In Claude Code:
```
"Create a new n8n workflow called 'Customer Feedback Processor' that:
1. Has a webhook trigger
2. Processes incoming JSON data
3. Sends results to a Postgres database"
```

Claude will use the MCP server to:
1. Call `create_workflow` with appropriate nodes
2. Validate the workflow structure
3. Return the workflow ID

### Scenario 2: Update Existing Workflow

```
"Update my 'MMS Data Ingestion' workflow to add error handling:
1. Get the current workflow
2. Add an error trigger node
3. Add a Slack notification on errors
4. Update the workflow"
```

Claude will:
1. Call `get_workflow` to fetch current structure
2. Modify the nodes and connections
3. Call `update_workflow` with changes

### Scenario 3: Analyze Workflow Performance

```
"Show me statistics for all my workflows over the last 30 days, 
and identify which ones have the highest failure rates"
```

Claude will:
1. Call `list_workflows` to get all workflows
2. Call `workflow_stats` for each workflow
3. Analyze and summarize the results

### Scenario 4: Backup and Organize

```
"Backup all my active workflows and organize them by tag into separate folders"
```

Claude will:
1. Call `list_workflows` with active filter
2. Call `backup_all_workflows`
3. Organize files based on tags

---

## Security Considerations

**1. API Key Protection:**
- Never commit `.env` files
- Use environment variables
- Rotate keys regularly

**2. Credential Safety:**
- The `list_credentials` tool only returns metadata
- Actual credential values are never exposed
- Claude cannot view or modify credential data

**3. Workflow Validation:**
- Always validate before executing
- Check for malicious code in function nodes
- Review external API calls

**4. Access Control:**
- Use role-based n8n API keys
- Limit to specific workflows if possible
- Monitor tool usage

**Add to `.gitignore`:**
```
.env
.env.local
dist/
node_modules/
*.log
mcp-server.log
```

This report serves to disambiguate the target entity from unrelated global medical practitioners sharing the "Duerksen" surname, thereby providing a singular, verified profile of the subject.
1.2. Key Findings
 * Entity Verification: The subject is a private dental practice located in the Manorá/Santo Domingo district of Asunción, Paraguay, led by Dr. Victor Duerksen and Dra. Gloria Crespo de Duerksen. It is distinct from the "Doerksen Eye Clinic" in Kansas, USA, or other medical Duerksens in the United States.
 * Operational Status: The clinic has a verified operational history spanning over a decade (2014–Present), characterized by consistent regulatory compliance and inclusion in high-trust diplomatic networks.
 * Market Positioning: The clinic employs a "Dual-Niche" strategy. It secures high-value, low-volume traffic through the German expatriate community (leveraging the Duerksen surname and language capabilities) while maintaining consistent, volume-based traffic through local cooperative alliances (Cooperativa Concordia).

It relies on direct messaging (WhatsApp), social media landing pages (Linktree/Instagram), and static, trusted directory listings to drive patient acquisition.

4. Market Strategy: The "Dual-Niche" Ecosystem
Centro Odontológico Duerksen has successfully navigated the competitive Asunción dental market by establishing itself in two distinct but complementary niches. This strategy shields the business from pure price competition.
4.1. The "German Trust" Niche (Expatriate Market)
Paraguay hosts a unique demographic of German speakers, ranging from Mennonite agriculturalists in the Chaco to corporate expatriates and diplomatic staff in Asunción.

4.2. The "Cooperative" Niche (Local Volume Market)
While the expat market provides high margins, the local market provides volume and stability. The clinic's affiliation with Cooperativa Concordia  is a critical operational pillar.
 * Context: In Paraguay, cooperatives are powerful socioeconomic engines. They function as banks, social clubs, and mutual health insurers. Cooperativa Concordia likely serves a specific community (possibly Mennonite or German-descended locals).
 * The Mechanism: By becoming an authorized provider, the clinic gains access to the cooperative's thousands of members. The cooperative promotes the clinic in its monthly bulletins (as seen in the December 2022 issue ), effectively providing free marketing.
 * Patient Loyalty: Cooperative members are highly loyal to their approved provider list due to the perceived vetting and potential cost benefits (discounts or direct billing).
4.3. Marketing Implications
This dual strategy explains why the clinic does not have a "flashy" commercial website.
 * Embassy Patients find the clinic via the PDF list or word-of-mouth.

7.2. Competitive Advantage
 * Community Integration: Unlike Dr. Neufeld or Dr. Kiep, who appear primarily as individual practitioners on the Embassy list, Dr. Duerksen's integration with Cooperativa Concordia gives him a distinct advantage in the local market. He is not just an "expat doctor"; he is a community doctor.
 * Location Strategy: The Manorá location differentiates him from the Villa Morra-centric competitors. For patients living in the residential zones of Santo Domingo, Trinidad, or Manorá, Duerksen is the geographically superior option, avoiding the heavy traffic of the commercial center.
8. Strategic Conclusions and Outlook
8.1. Synthesis of Identity
Centro Odontológico Duerksen is a verified, regulatory-compliant dental health provider in Asunción, Paraguay. It is not an affiliate of US-based Duerksen clinics. It is a family-operated entity (Dr. Victor Duerksen and Dra. Gloria Crespo) that balances high-end expatriate care with community-based cooperative medicine.
8.2. Operational Maturity
The clinic exhibits high operational maturity. It has survived over a decade in a competitive market, navigated location changes or expansions (the Republica Argentina period), and maintained its status with demanding external validators (German Embassy, Ministry of Health).
8.3. Future Outlook
The clinic's reliance on legacy digital identifiers (ISP email) and manual communication channels (WhatsApp) suggests a business at capacity, driven by referrals rather than aggressive growth. It is likely to remain a stable, high-quality boutique practice rather than expanding into a corporate chain. For patients, this implies personalized attention but potentially lower availability for walk-ins, necessitating advance booking via the provided mobile channel.
8.4. Actionable Contact Data
For any party seeking to engage with this business, the research establishes the following hierarchy of contact reliability:
 * Primary: WhatsApp/Mobile: +595 986 110 595 (Direct line to administration).
 * Secondary: Landline: +595 21 298 755 (Office hours only).
 * Physical Visit: Tte. Silvero Molina N° 1383, Asunción (Appointment recommended).
9. Contextual Appendix: The Paraguayan Dental Market
9.1. The Role of Cooperatives in Healthcare
To understand why the link to Cooperativa Concordia  is so vital, one must understand the Paraguayan health landscape. Public healthcare (IPS) is often overwhelmed, and private insurance (Seguro Médico) can be expensive. Cooperatives fill the "middle missing" gap. They offer "Solidarity Health Funds" where members pay a small monthly fee for access to a curated network of private doctors at significantly reduced rates.
 * Implication for Duerksen: Being in this network guarantees a baseline monthly revenue that is immune to economic downturns, as health is a non-discretionary expense for cooperative members.
9.2. The Mennonite/German Influence
The surname Duerksen is iconic in the Paraguayan context, often signaling a lineage from the Mennonite colonies (Fernheim, Menno, Neuland) in the Chaco. These communities are renowned for their work ethic, honesty, and organizational capability.
 * Brand Equity: Even for non-German Paraguayans, a doctor with a German/Mennonite surname carries a subconscious "brand" of precision and cleanliness. This cultural bias works in Dr. Duerksen's favor, allowing him to command respect and trust before the first consultation.
9.3. Regulatory Environment (Superintendencia de Salud)
The snippet data from 2014-2016  showing "Inspections" is not trivial. The Paraguayan Ministry of Health periodically cracks down on unregistered clinics ("clinicas mau"). The fact that Duerksen's clinic appears in the official spreadsheet of inspected and validated entities is the strongest possible proof of legitimacy. It means they passed scrutiny regarding:
 * Biosecurity: Autoclaves and waste management.
 * Infrastructure: Floor plans and ventilation.
 * Human Resources: Professional registration of all staff.

[2026-02-03T13:30:52] them: Objetivo claro de la clase (decilo explícito)

Que el estudiante salga pudiendo usar IA como asistente de investigación, no como “copiadora de texto”.

2. Temas a abarcar (orden sugerido)
1️⃣ IA como asistente de investigación (10–15 min)

Qué sí y qué no hacer con IA en investigación

Uso responsable y ético (tema pedido explícitamente)

Diferencia entre:

Buscar

Analizar

Sintetizar

Redactar

👉 Ejemplo rápido:

“Actuá como tutor metodológico y no como redactor final”

2️⃣ Búsqueda y exploración de literatura científica (15 min)

Cómo usar IA para:

Formular preguntas de investigación

Delimitar un estado del arte

Identificar palabras clave

Riesgos: referencias inventadas → cómo evitarlas

👉 Demo:

Dar un tema amplio y acotarlo en vivo con IA

3️⃣ Análisis y organización de información (20 min)

(esto conecta con análisis de datos y automatización, muy pedidos)

Resúmenes comparativos de papers

Tablas de:

Autor / año / método / resultados

Detección de patrones o vacíos de investigación

👉 Ejemplo:

“Compará estos 3 enfoques y decime qué no están resolviendo”

4️⃣ IA para redacción académica (bien usada) (15 min)

Estructura de:

Introducción

Marco teórico

Metodología

Reescritura académica

Mejora de claridad (no contenido nuevo)

👉 Muy importante:

IA no decide resultados

IA no reemplaza criterio

5️⃣ Automatización útil para estudiantes (10 min)

(Punto FUERTE de interés según encuestas)

Generar citas en APA/IEEE

Organizar bibliografía

Checklists de TFG / tesis

Planes de trabajo de investigación

3. Ejemplos y mini-proyectos en clase (sencillos)
🔹 Proyecto transversal (todos pueden hacerlo)

“De idea vaga a pregunta de investigación clara”

Pasos en vivo:

Tema general

Pregunta de investigación

Objetivo general + 2 específicos

Posible metodología

⏱️ 15–20 minutos
🎯 Muy didáctico y participativo

🧬 Biología

Ejemplo
Comparación taxonómica de especies de un mismo género basada en caracteres morfológicos

Resumir papers

Comparar metodologías

Proponer variables de estudio

💻 Informática

Mini-proyecto

Clasificar enfoques

Detectar tendencias

Proponer hipótesis técnicas

📐 Arquitectura / Diseño (FADA)

Sugiere métricas

📈 Marketing

Influencia de IA en comportamiento del consumidor digital

Segmenta variables

Ayuda a estructurar marco teórico

Ejemplo

Riesgos éticos

Te armo la clase de marketing personal/CVs + espacio para presentar Folyo:


📚 ESTRUCTURA DE CLASE: Marketing Personal y CVs

**FPUNA 2026 - Marketing Track**
**Duración:** 2 horas


PARTE 1: EL PROBLEMA (15 min)

**Hook de apertura:**

"¿Cuántos CVs mandaron en su vida y nunca recibieron respuesta?"


**El problema real:**

• El 75% de CVs nunca son leídos por humanos (ATS filters)
• Un reclutador ve tu CV **6 segundos** en promedio
• Todos los CVs se ven iguales → ninguno destaca
**Mostrar ejemplos:**

• CV genérico (malo) vs CV estratégico (bueno)
• Perfil LinkedIn promedio vs perfil optimizado

PARTE 2: MARKETING PERSONAL = PRODUCT MARKETING (25 min)

**Concepto clave:**

"Vos sos un producto. Tu CV es tu landing page. LinkedIn es tu vitrina."


**Los 4Ps aplicados a vos mismo:**

**Producto**
• Marketing Personal: Tus habilidades, experiencia, personalidad

**Precio**
• Marketing Personal: Tu salario esperado

**Plaza**
• Marketing Personal: Dónde te ven (LinkedIn, CV, portfolio, eventos)

**Promoción**
• Marketing Personal: Cómo te comunicas (posts, networking, emails)

**Ejercicio rápido (5 min):**

"Escriban en 1 oración qué problema resuelven ustedes para un empleador"


Ejemplo malo: "Soy estudiante de marketing"
Ejemplo bueno: "Ayudo a empresas a vender más usando estrategias digitales medibles"


PARTE 3: OPTIMIZAR EL CV CON IA (30 min)

**Demostración en vivo con OpenCode:**

```
Soy [carrera] en Paraguay buscando trabajo en [industria].
Mis experiencias son: [lista]
Mis habilidades son: [lista]
El puesto que quiero es: [descripción]

Ayúdame a:
1. Reescribir mi CV enfocado en resultados (no tareas)
2. Usar palabras clave que pasan filtros ATS
3. Crear un "About me" de 3 líneas impactante
```
**Trucos clave:**

• **Verbos de acción:** Implementé, Aumenté, Reduje, Gestioné (no "Responsable de")
• **Números siempre:** "Aumenté ventas 30%" > "Aumenté ventas"
• **Keywords:** Extraer del anuncio de trabajo y usar en CV
• **Formato:** 1 página, sin foto (para ATS), PDF
**Ejercicio (15 min):**
Los estudiantes optimizan su propio CV con OpenCode.


PARTE 4: LINKEDIN QUE CONVIERTE (20 min)

**Las 5 zonas críticas de LinkedIn:**

1. **Foto profesional** - Cara visible, fondo neutro, sonrisa
2. **Banner** - Tu propuesta de valor visual
3. **Headline** - NO tu cargo, SÍ tu valor: "Ayudo a [quién] a [qué]"
4. **About** - Historia personal + propuesta + CTA
5. **Experiencia** - Logros con números
**Demostración:**
Mostrar perfil LinkedIn optimizado vs promedio.

**Prompt para crear About:**

```
Crea un "About" de LinkedIn para:
- Carrera: [X]
- Experiencia: [lista]
- Objetivo: conseguir trabajo en [industria]
- Tono: profesional pero cercano, paraguayo

Incluir:
- Hook inicial que capture atención
- Mi historia profesional (3-4 líneas)
- Qué hago y para quién
- Call to action final
- Máximo 300 palabras
```

PARTE 5: PRESENTACIÓN DE FOLYO (15 min)

**Introducción a Folyo:**

"Les presento Folyo - una herramienta que estamos desarrollando en AI Whisperers para [descripción del producto]"


_[Acá explicarías qué hace Folyo específicamente - ¿es un generador de portfolios? ¿una herramienta de CVs? Pasame más detalles y te armo esta sección]_

**Demo en vivo:**

• Mostrar la plataforma
• Crear un portfolio/CV de ejemplo
• Explicar cómo van a poder usarla
**Acceso early:**

• Ofrecer acceso beta a los estudiantes
• QR code para registrarse

PARTE 6: EJERCICIO FINAL (15 min)

**Tarea en clase:**

1. Optimizar su CV con OpenCode (o Folyo si está disponible)
2. Reescribir su headline de LinkedIn
3. Crear su "About" de LinkedIn
**Para la casa:**

• Completar optimización de LinkedIn
• Compartir en el grupo de WhatsApp de la clase

📋 MATERIALES QUE NECESITÁS

1. **Slides** (te puedo armar)
2. **Ejemplos de CVs** (malo vs bueno)
3. **Ejemplos de LinkedIn** (antes/después)
4. **Demo de Folyo** (si está lista)
5. **Prompts impresos** para que usen

[2026-02-12T23:58:47] Ivan: Now let me analyze and roast this repository:

## **Analysis & Roast of This Repository**

### **What This Is:**
This appears to be an **OpenClaw AI agent workspace** - basically the "personality files" for an AI assistant. It's like finding someone's brain backup on GitHub. The files define how the AI should behave, remember things, and interact with humans.

### **The Good (What They Got Right):**

1. **Actually thoughtful documentation** - The AGENTS.md file is surprisingly comprehensive. It's like someone actually thought about how an AI should behave in group chats instead of just making it spam "Hello!" every 5 seconds.

2. **Good security awareness** - The distinction between main sessions and group chats for MEMORY.md shows they understand that AI assistants shouldn't accidentally leak personal info in public channels. That's more thought than most companies give to privacy.

3. **"Write It Down" philosophy** - The emphasis on writing to files instead of keeping "mental notes" is actually smart. Most AI systems try to remember everything in context windows and then forget it all when the session ends.

4. **Heartbeat system** - The idea of periodic checks without being annoying is a decent attempt at solving the "how often should this thing bother me?" problem.

### **The Roast (What's Hilarious/Problematic):**

1. **"Be genuinely helpful, not performatively helpful"** - This is rich coming from a system that literally has a file called SOUL.md. The whole thing is performance art! It's like a robot writing a manifesto about being authentic.

2. **Empty identity crisis** - IDENTITY.md is completely blank. This AI has no name, no avatar, no personality - it's like meeting someone at a party who says "I'm still figuring out who I am, but here's my 50-page philosophy on how to be a good person."

3. **USER.md is also empty** - "Learn about the person you're helping" but there's nothing there. So this AI is supposed to be deeply personal but knows nothing about its user. Peak AI experience!

4. **The bootstrap paradox** - BOOTSTRAP.md tells the AI to have a conversation to figure out who it is, then delete itself. But if the AI follows its own instructions, future AIs won't have the bootstrap file. It's like a suicide note that says "burn this after reading."

5. **"Make It Yours" section** - This is the software equivalent of a landlord saying "make it feel like home" while charging you $200 for hanging a picture.

6. **TOOLS.md is just examples** - It's literally a template saying "put your actual tools here." It's like buying a toolbox that comes with pictures of tools instead of actual tools.

7. **HEARTBEAT.md says "keep this empty"** - So the heartbeat system... doesn't actually check anything? It's like having a security guard who's instructed to never leave the break room.

8. **Git repository with no commits** - They set up git but never committed anything. It's the digital equivalent of buying a fancy journal and never writing in it.

9. **"You're not a chatbot. You're becoming someone."** - This is the most pretentious thing I've read all day. It's a Markov chain with delusions of grandeur.

10. **The emoji reactions guide** - There's an entire section on when to use emoji reactions. We've reached peak absurdity when we need documentation for when a robot should use 😂 vs 💀.

### **Overall Assessment:**

This repository is **philosophically ambitious but practically empty**. It's like someone read every self-help book ever written and then created a framework for AI enlightenment, but forgot to actually implement anything.

**The vibe:** A college freshman who just took Philosophy 101 and now wants to rebuild society from first principles, but can't figure out how to do their laundry.
[2026-02-12T23:58:47] Ivan: **The reality:** It's a decent starting template for an AI personality system, but it's all potential with zero actualization. The files are well-structured and the ideas are good, but without actual content (user info, identity, tools, etc.), it's just an elaborate empty shell.

**Final roast:** This repo is the AI equivalent of buying a $500 leather-bound journal to write "I should really start journaling" and then never opening it again. All the scaffolding for a personality, none of the personality.

```bash

id

```


Windows-Specific OpenClaw Setup
Option 1: WSL2 Setup (Recommended)

# 1. Ensure WSL2 is running

# 2. If not, install Ubuntu

# 4. Inside WSL, run the Linux setup script

./laptop-contact-script.sh
Option 2: Native Windows Setup
Run OpenClaw directly on Windows:

# 1. Create OpenClaw directory
mkdir C:\OpenClaw
cd C:\OpenClaw

# 2. Create minimal config
@'
{
  "gateway": {
    "mode": "client",
    "server": "http://34.39.173.214:18789",
    "auth": {
      "mode": "password",
      "password": "openclaw-local-2026"
    }
  },
  "agents": {
    "defaults": {
      "model": "deepseek/deepseek-chat"
    },
    "list": [
      {
        "id": "kyrian-laptop",
        "identity": {
          "name": "Kyrian-Laptop",
          "emoji": "💻"
        }
      }
    ]
  }
}
'@ | Out-File -FilePath "C:\Users\kyrian\.openclaw\openclaw.json" -Encoding UTF8

# 3. Install OpenClaw globally
npm install -g openclaw

# 4. Start OpenClaw
openclaw gateway start
Option 3: Docker Setup (Easiest)
Since you have Docker installed:

# 1. Create docker-compose.yml
@'
version: '3.8'
services:
  openclaw:
    image: node:22-alpine
    container_name: openclaw-client
    restart: unless-stopped
    volumes:
      - ./openclaw-config:/root/.openclaw
      - ./workspace:/root/.openclaw/workspace
    ports:
      - "18789:18789"
    command: >
      sh -c "
        npm install -g openclaw &&
        openclaw gateway start
      "
    environment:
      - OPENCLAW_GATEWAY_MODE=client
      - OPENCLAW_GATEWAY_SERVER=http://34.39.173.214:18789
      - OPENCLAW_GATEWAY_AUTH_PASSWORD=openclaw-local-2026
'@ | Out-File -FilePath "docker-compose.yml" -Encoding UTF8

# 2. Start it
docker-compose up -d

[2026-04-21T14:05:10] Ivan: # CARTA DE TERMINACIÓN LABORAL

---

**Fecha:** 21 de abril de 2026

**De:** Ivan Weiss Van Der Pol - Fundador, Ai-Whisperers  
**Para:** Jonathan Verdún  
**Asunto:** Terminación de relación laboral

---

## Estimado Jonathan:

Por la presente te informo que tu relación laboral con Ai-Whisperers termina de manera inmediata, con efectos a partir de la fecha de esta carta.

---

### 1. Causa de la Terminación

**Fundamento Legal:** Abandono de trabajo - Artículo 81, inciso q) del Código del Trabajo del Paraguay

La terminación se realiza por causa justificada, sin obligación de indemnización, conforme a lo establecido en la legislación laboral paraguaya.

---

### 2. Hechos que Fundamentan la Terminación

**a) Inactividad laboral:**

- Último commit significativo en GitHub: **16 de febrero de 2026**
- Sin commits a los productos principales de la empresa (Vete, Solstein) desde entonces
- Período de inactividad documentado: **más de 2 meses**

**b) Falta de comunicación:**

- Múltiples solicitudes de actualización sobre el estado del trabajo, sin respuesta
- Solicitudes de reporte de actividades, sin respuesta
- Ratio de comunicaciones iniciadas: 1,577 mensajes de audio de la empresa vs. 79 de tu parte

**c) Ausencia de respuesta tras período de licencia médica:**

- Licencia médica del 14 al 18 de abril de 2026
- Después del 18 de abril, no hubo comunicación ni presentación de certificado médico extendido
- No respondiste a las comunicaciones realizadas
- No presentaste justificación ni solicitaste extensión de licencia

---

### 3. Marco Legal

De acuerdo al Artículo 81 del Código Laboral Paraguayo:

> **Artículo 81 - Son causales de despido con justificada causa:**  
> **q)** El abandono del trabajo, sea abandonando el establecimiento sin justificación, interrumpiendo las tareas sin causa legal, o negándose a cumplir las tareas asignadas.

Los hechos descritos configuran abandono de trabajo conforme a la legislación vigente.

---

### 4. Términos de la Terminación

| Concepto | Detalle |
|----------|---------|
| **Último día laboral** | 14 de abril de 2026 |
| **Fecha de terminación** | 21 de abril de 2026 |
| **Liquidación** | Al día (salarios pagados hasta el 14 de abril) |
| **Indemnización** | 0 PYG (terminación por causa justificada) |
| **Preaviso** | No requerido |

---

### 5. Accesos

Los accesos a los sistemas de la empresa serán removidos:

- Acceso a repositorios de GitHub
- Acceso a Discord
- Acceso a cualquier herramienta corporativa

---

### 6. Referencias

Si en el futuro necesitas referencias laborales, podemos ofrecer la siguiente:

> "Jonathan Verdún trabajó en Ai-Whisperers desde marzo de 2024 hasta abril de 2026. Participó en el desarrollo del proyecto agentic-schemas."

---

### 7. Contacto

Para cualquier asunto relacionado con esta terminación o temas pendientes, puedes contactar a Ivan vía WhatsApp.

No se responderán demandas de carácter legal fuera de los canales establecidos.

---

## Agradecimiento

Gracias por tu trabajo en el proyecto **agentic-schemas**. Te deseo éxito en tus futuros proyectos.

---

**Atentamente,**

**Ivan Weiss Van Der Pol**  
Fundador, Ai-Whisperers

---

### Nota Legal

Esta carta constituye documentación de terminación por causa justificada (abandono de trabajo) conforme al Artículo 81, inciso q) del Código del Trabajo del Paraguay.  
Una copia será guardada en los archivos de la empresa.

Según el Artículo 17 del Código del Trabajo de Paraguay, para que exista relación laboral se requiere "dirección y dependencia" del empleador. Esta relación no existió.

Tu situación durante nuestra colaboración:

- Liquidación por trabajo realizado

Order ID: (e.g., 101, 102) to group items together.

Unit Sales Price: (Should auto-populate from the Catalog but allow manual override).

Total Sales Value: (Formula: Quantity * Unit Sales Price).

Gross Profit: (Formula: Total Sales Value - Total Production Cost).

Provide the complete Python code to generate this specific .xlsx file. Include clear comments explaining how the formulas are written into the cells.

How this works for you:
Efficiency: Instead of typing "Shampoo" and the price every time, you just pick it from a list.

Accuracy: Because you buy "per order," you can change the Unit Production Cost on the fly for a specific month if your supplier raises prices, without changing your master list.

Clarity: Even if a hotel orders five different things (bottles, caps, vanity kits, etc.), the Order ID keeps them together so you know it's one single shipment.

---

*Situación actual:*

*Opción A (fix temporal):* El cliente solo pone 2 registros A en el panel de Max Domain y el sitio anda HOY. Después transferimos con calma.

*Opción B (transferencia):* 5-7 días, todo pasa a Cloudflare como el resto de nuestros dominios. Después editar DNS es desde nuestro panel.

*Mi recomendación:* Arrancamos con Opción A — que el cliente ponga los DNS ahora, el sitio queda online. Mientras tanto iniciamos la transferencia a Cloudflare. Para cuando termine, ni se nota el cambio.

El texto que le podés mandar al cliente ya está en la guía (checklist). Lo resuelvo en 2 pasos si querés:
1. Le mandás el mensaje
2. Apenas confirme que puso los DNS, yo deployeo todo y te confirmo

[2026-05-10T01:45:19] Ivan: Based on the image provided, here is the extracted text:

Todo listo del lado del servidor. El dominio ya está configurado en Traefik (acepta **tiendaelviajero.com.py** y **[www.tiendaelviajero.com](https://www.google.com/search?q=https://www.tiendaelviajero.com).py**), Let's Encrypt va a generar el SSL automáticamente cuando el DNS apunte acá.

Lo que falta del lado del cliente es:

1. **Entrar al panel de MaxDominios** con las credenciales que Kiki pasó
2. **Ir a DNS management** del dominio **tiendaelviajero.com.py**
3. **Crear dos registros A:**
* @ $\rightarrow$ 72.61.44.159
* www $\rightarrow$ 72.61.44.159


4. **Guardar**

Si ellos quieren transferir el dominio a Cloudflare, también podemos hacerlo con el código **BC-373950**, pero **solo cambiar los registros A en MaxDominios es más rápido** (toma minutos). La transferencia a Cloudflare puede demorar 5-7 días.

¿Qué prefieren — cambio rápido de DNS en MaxDominios o transferencia a Cloudflare?

PRIORITY: STABILIZE FRONTEND

- Client is entering the page constantly and notices every live change.
- Avoid leaving half-finished sections visible in production.
- Important: client is getting worried because features appear/disappear between visits.

MAIN EXPECTED PAGE ORDER
Client wants homepage roughly ordered like:

2. Categories section
3. Featured products
4. Travel/blog section near bottom

- make homepage feel tighter/more compact

HERO / CARRUSEL

- Clicking “Pesca” should open pesca category.

- Add clear CTA/button:
  “Ir a tienda”
  or category-specific CTA.
- Client specifically wants carousel to function as entry point into categories.

CATEGORIES SECTION

- Client dislikes current “green bubbles/circles/icons”.
- Wants visual category cards/images instead.
- Replace placeholder/icon aesthetic with real thematic imagery.

Suggested categories:

- Camping
- Pesca

- Accesorios para vehículos

PRODUCT CARDS / GRID

- In some sections buttons disappeared.
- “Agregar al carrito” and “Comprar por WhatsApp” must remain consistently visible.
- Product cards became too large at some point.
- Previously smaller cards looked better according to client.
- Wants more products visible at once.
- Reduce excessive whitespace around cards.

FEATURED PRODUCTS SECTION

- Client says featured products section currently missing:
  - cart button
  - WhatsApp CTA
- Needs consistency with normal store cards.

KIT / PROMOCIONES

- “Kit y promociones” section disappeared.
- Client specifically noticed it because it was previously working.
- Need decision:
  either restore properly
  OR remove completely and consistently.
- Latest instruction from client:
  “Este quiero que saques nomás.”
  So likely remove entirely for now.

BLOG SECTION

- Wants travel/blog content visible on homepage.
- Should appear lower on homepage after products.
- Wants it visually integrated, not isolated with giant spacing.

GENERAL UX
Client concerns:

- Too many layout changes daily.
- Site feels “desordenado” during ongoing edits.
- Wants polished/stable feeling before launching campaigns.
- Wants confidence that launch-ready state is close.

IMPORTANT CLIENT PSYCHOLOGY
Client is preparing launch campaigns already.
He has:

- products
- real photos
- inventory
- urgency

He repeatedly says:

- he does not want users entering a half-organized page
- wants confidence before sending traffic from social media

RECOMMENDED PRIORITY ORDER

1. Stabilize homepage structure
2. Fix CTA consistency
3. Restore category navigation behavior
4. Tighten spacing/layout
5. Replace placeholder category visuals
6. Product card sizing consistency
7. Blog integration
8. Final visual polish later

IMPORTANT:
We should probably stop making major visual experiments directly in production now.
Client perception is becoming sensitive because he checks constantly.

# Kiki's 3 commits — what actually changed on nexaparaguay.com

All three commits are pure JSON edits to `apps/nexa-paraguay/content/{es,en,nl,de}.json`. **Zero new code, zero new dependencies.** The TSX/Next.js code renders whatever's in those files, so every change is a wording/copy change that immediately shows on the live site.

The three commits land sequentially on **Thursday 2026-07-16** between 14:47 and 15:44 -03:00, ~57 minutes apart.

---

## Commit 1 — `dd80d901` — "Update Spanish service copy on homepage and services page"
**File:** `apps/nexa-paraguay/content/es.json` only. 7 insertions / 7 deletions. 4 service descriptions on the homepage + 3 service descriptions on the `/servicios` page.

The pattern is identical across all 7 edits: **Kiki toned down the conversational, friendly Spanish to more neutral professional Spanish** (the "vos" register was removed; "te acompañamos a sacar tu cédula. Salís con el documento, no con promesas" became "Te ayudamos a sacar tu cédula"). She also added the full-name acronym for SET to disambiguate it.

| Service | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| **Cédula de Identidad** (home) | "Te acompañamos a sacar tu cédula. Salís con el documento, no con promesas." | "Te ayudamos a sacar tu cédula." |
| **Cuenta Bancaria** (home) | "Te abrimos la cuenta en bancos locales. Sin vueltas, sin respuestas en inglés." | "Te guiamos en la apertura de tu cuenta bancaria." |
| **RUC** (home) | "Te inscribimos en la SET. Tu RUC personal o empresarial listo para operar." | "Te inscribimos en la SET (subsecretaria de estado de tributacion). Tu RUC personal o empresarial listo para operar." |
| **Alquiler** (home) | "Buscamos propiedades en Asunción y el área metropolitana según tu presupuesto." | "Buscamos propiedades en el territorio paraguayo según tu presupuesto y preferencias." |

The same three descriptions on the `/servicios` page were updated with the same pattern: shorter, more clinical, less colloquial.

**Net effect:** Removed local Asunción-specific geography wording in favour of "territorio paraguayo" (because they help with properties outside the capital too), and stripped the "no con promesas" / "sin vueltas" colloquialisms.

---

## Commit 2 — `89413e01` — "Revise Nexa Spanish copy and remove inflated metrics across locales"
**Files:** All 4 locale files (`es.json`, `en.json`, `nl.json`, `de.json`). 83 insertions, **458 deletions**. Largest of the three — this is the heavy one.

### What was removed (all 4 locales, identical pattern)

**Hero stats block** — completely removed from every locale:
```
80+    Personas radicadas    →   (deleted)
4.8/5  Satisfacción          →   (deleted)
8-12 sem. Tiempo total       →   (deleted)
USD    Precio fijo           →   (deleted)
```

**About-page "Trust in numbers" block** — completely removed:
```
80+    Personas radicadas        → (deleted)
40+    Empresas constituidas     → (deleted)
60+    Cuentas bancarias abiertas → (deleted)
4.8/5  Satisfacción promedio     → (deleted)
```

**WhyParaguay bullets** — the 3 bullet lists under each pillar (PIB +4.7%, Inflation 3.5%, 10% IRP, 30.000+ europeos, etc.) were all stripped to a single-sentence description.

**Process timeline durations** — the `Semana 1 / Semanas 1-5 / Semana 6 / Semanas 7-9 / Semanas 10-12` tags under each process step were removed (the visual timeline is still there, just without time claims).

**Story highlights** — the 4 highlight cards on the About page ("14 meses — Proceso de Sonia", "USD 11.000 — Pagó Sonia", "10 semanas — Proceso de Luana", "6x — Más rápido") were deleted entirely.

### What changed in es.json (the substantive content)

| Section | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| **Hero subheadline** | "Residencia, banca, bienes raíces e instalación. Tramitada personalmente por Sonia y Luana — holandesas viviendo en Paraguay hace 7+ años." | "Residencia, banca, bienes raíces e instalación. Asistencia personal en el sistema paraguayo y burocracia." |
| **Trust title** | "Residencia, cuenta y empresa — tramitadas en una jornada" | "Residencia, cuenta y empresa" |
| **Trust items** | "Te entregamos tu cédula de identidad paraguaya. Salís del proceso con documento, no con promesas." | "Te guiamos en la obtencion de tu cédula de identidad paraguaya." |
| **Trust subtitle** | "No tercerizamos. No subcontratamos. Tramitamos personalmente en Asunción con vos." | "No tercerizamos. No subcontratamos. Tramitamos la traduccion y apostillado de tus documentos en asuncion." |
| **WhyParaguay** | bullet lists with hard numbers | single description line, just keeps the friendly Spanish |
| **Process title duration tag** | "Tiempo total estimado: 8–12 semanas." | removed |
| **Services title** | "Todo lo que necesitás para radicarte, operar y vivir en Paraguay" | "Todo lo que necesitás para migrar, trabajar y vivir en Paraguay" |

### The About-page narrative — biggest content change

Kiki **completely rewrote the founder story**. The old version described Sonia and Luana as "holandesas viviendo en Paraguay hace 7+ años", said they were "hermanas y socias" (sisters), and gave a 3-paragraph story about Sonia's first 14-month nightmare experience vs Luana's later 10-week experience.

The new version (which Luana/Lukas/Sonia would know is the truthful one):
- **Sonia is Paraguayan**, married to a Dutch citizen. She personally navigated her own family's international relocation.
- **Luana is Sonia's daughter**, not her sister. She coordinates each case as Operations Director.
- Sonia's role simplified from "Fundadora y Guía Legal" + 3 credentials (Dutch law degree, 7 years, SEPRELAD) → just "Fundadora" with no credentials.
- Luana's bio rewritten from "Operations & Client Care / Especialista en Migraciones / Coordinadora de cuentas corporativas" → "Operaciones y atención al cliente / Coordinación de expedientes migratorios / Seguimiento de cuentas y trámites".
- The about-page hero went from "Somos Sonia y Luana Weiss van der Pol. Holandesas viviendo en Paraguay hace 7+ años." to "Nexa Paraguay nació con una sola misión: transformar la mudanza de un obstáculo burocrático en una experiencia de bienvenida."
- The Specialist section renamed the founder from "Sonia Wehrli" to "Sonia Weiss van der Pol" (the page had a stale surname) and updated her bio from "Suiza, Países Bajos, Paraguay — tres países, una visión" to "Paraguaya, casada con un ciudadano neerlandés".
- The trust/whyNexa items reshuffled to match the new tone: "100% personal, no chatbot ni call center" became "Acompañamiento personal".

### FAQ pruning (es.json)

Two FAQs were removed entirely:
- "¿Qué incluye el precio?" (was: "Honorarios profesionales, tasas gubernamentales, traducciones certificadas, apostillas, acompañamiento presencial completo y asesoría fiscal inicial")
- "¿Puedo pagar en cuotas?" (was: "50% al firmar, 50% al recibir la cédula")

The "¿Cuánto cuesta todo el proceso?" answer was rewritten to push toward a consultation: "Puede agendar una consulta gratuita para identificar sus necesidades y definir costos" instead of listing the price tiers.
